// The language has both an interpreter and a compiler: robix and robixc. Both are written in Robix.
// robixc translates each program from Robix to RXASM, then from RXASM to machine code. No clang++, LLVM, or C++ runtime remains in that path.
// A compiled program must run faster than idiomatic C and C++ on the Robix benchmark suite, and it must give the same control over hardware.
// One compiler, one assembly language, one encoder. Linux x86-64 is the first target. Windows x86-64 is the same compiler with a different target record.
// Add clear examples in the Examples folder for every feature or syntax that is implemented.

# Robix Built-in Package Catalogue
## Proposed Standard Library and SDK Package Names

This catalogue is a proposed package namespace specification for Robix. It consolidates the previously proposed built-in packages, corrects acronym capitalization, and adds a dedicated algorithms catalogue.

**Naming convention**
- Package segments use PascalCase for ordinary words: `Robix.Collections`, `Robix.TimeZones`.
- Well-established acronyms retain their conventional uppercase spelling: `HTTP`, `HTTPS`, `TLS`, `SSH`, `TCP`, `UDP`, `DNS`, `JSON`, `XML`, `SQL`, `GPIO`, `I2C`, `SPI`, `UART`, `PWM`, `USB`, `PCI`, `AI`, `ONNX`, `PDF`, `QR`, `HTML`, `CSS`, `DOM`, `ORM`, `FFI`, `API`, `CLI`.
- Conventional mixed-case names remain conventional where appropriate: `WebSocket`, `gRPC`, `WiFi`, `NoSQL`.
- Package availability does not imply that every platform has the required hardware, native library, codec, accelerator, or system service.

## 3. Algorithms and data structures
# **ROBIX — Algorithms and Data Structures API Specification**

29 packages · Classes, interfaces, structures, algorithms, functions, complexity requirements and implementation rules

This is the proposed **Robix Algorithms Standard Library (RASL)**, covering all 29 packages you provided.

For each package, I will define:

1. **Classes and types:** Classes, structs, enums, interfaces, generic types, and supporting objects.
2. **Functions and methods:** The operations, algorithms, and utilities developers should be able to call.
3. **Advanced functionality:** Additional algorithms required for professional software development.
4. **Implementation contracts:** Type safety, memory usage, mutability, complexity, stability, error handling, and deterministic behavior.

The objective is a comprehensive API specification covering traditional algorithms, modern algorithms, scientific computing, AI, robotics, systems programming, and high-performance computing.

This is a proposed design, not a list of already implemented Robix APIs. It can become the foundation for defining actual compiler-checked function signatures.

## **4.&#x20;**`Robix.Algorithms.Selection`

## **14.&#x20;**`Robix.Algorithms.Cryptography`

**Responsibility:** Cryptographic algorithm interfaces and integrations with reviewed cryptographic implementations.

**Architecture:** This package should expose vetted algorithms and generic cryptographic interfaces. Higher-level key management, certificate validation, authentication, and TLS handling belong under `Robix.Security`.

### **Classes and types**

- `CryptographicAlgorithm`
- `CryptographicProvider`
- `CryptographicContext`
- `CryptographicPolicy`
- `CryptographicCapabilities`
- `CryptographicResult<T>`
- `CryptographicError`
- `Cipher`
- `SymmetricCipher`
- `AuthenticatedCipher`
- `CipherKey`
- `CipherNonce`
- `CipherTag`
- `CipherParameters`
- `HashAlgorithm`
- `MessageAuthenticationCode`
- `KeyDerivationFunction`
- `PasswordHashFunction`
- `DigitalSignature`
- `SignatureVerifier`
- `PublicKey`
- `PrivateKey`
- `KeyPair`
- `KeyAgreement`
- `KeyEncapsulationMechanism`
- `SecureRandomGenerator`
- `SecureBuffer`
- `CryptographicAlgorithmRegistry`

### **Algorithm implementations**

| Class / Interface  | Purpose                                      |
| ------------------ | -------------------------------------------- |
| `AES`              | Advanced Encryption Standard                 |
| `AESGCM`           | Authenticated AES encryption                 |
| `AESCCM`           | AES Counter with CBC-MAC                     |
| `ChaCha20Poly1305` | Authenticated stream encryption              |
| `SHA256`           | SHA-256 hash                                 |
| `SHA384`           | SHA-384 hash                                 |
| `SHA512`           | SHA-512 hash                                 |
| `SHA3`             | SHA-3 family                                 |
| `SHAKE`            | Extendable-output hash                       |
| `HMAC`             | Keyed message authentication                 |
| `KMAC`             | Keccak message authentication                |
| `HKDF`             | Key derivation                               |
| `PBKDF2`           | Password-based key derivation                |
| `Argon2id`         | Password hashing                             |
| `Scrypt`           | Memory-hard password derivation              |
| `Bcrypt`           | Password hashing                             |
| `RSAOAEP`          | RSA-based encryption                         |
| `RSAPSS`           | RSA signatures                               |
| `ECDSA`            | Elliptic-curve digital signatures            |
| `Ed25519`          | Edwards-curve signatures                     |
| `X25519`           | Elliptic-curve key agreement                 |
| `MLKEM`            | Post-quantum key encapsulation               |
| `MLDSA`            | Post-quantum signatures                      |
| `SLHDSA`           | Stateless hash-based post-quantum signatures |

### **Functions**

**Encryption**

```
Encrypt(plaintext, key, options)
Decrypt(ciphertext, key, options)
EncryptAuthenticated(plaintext, key, nonce, associatedData)
DecryptAuthenticated(ciphertext, key, nonce, tag, associatedData)
GenerateNonce(algorithm)
ValidateCiphertext(ciphertext, parameters)
```

**Hashing**

```
Hash(data, algorithm)
HashStream(stream, algorithm)
HashFile(path, algorithm)
SHA256(data)
SHA384(data)
SHA512(data)
SHA3(data)
ConstantTimeEquals(a, b)
```

**Authentication**

```
ComputeHMAC(key, data, algorithm)
VerifyHMAC(key, data, tag, algorithm)
ComputeKMAC(key, data, options)
VerifyAuthenticationTag(expected, actual)
```

**Key operations**

```
GenerateKey(algorithm)
GenerateKeyPair(algorithm)
DeriveKey(material, salt, options)
HKDFExtract(salt, inputKeyMaterial)
HKDFExpand(prk, info, length)
DeriveSharedSecret(privateKey, publicKey)
Encapsulate(publicKey)
Decapsulate(privateKey, ciphertext)
```

**Signatures**

```
Sign(data, privateKey, algorithm)
Verify(data, signature, publicKey, algorithm)
SignStream(stream, privateKey, algorithm)
VerifyStream(stream, signature, publicKey, algorithm)
```

**Passwords**

```
HashPassword(password, options)
VerifyPassword(password, encodedHash)
NeedsRehash(encodedHash, policy)
GeneratePasswordSalt()
```

**Security requirements:** Cryptographic functions must use secure randomness, explicit key ownership, authenticated encryption by default, correct nonce handling, and constant-time operations where required. Legacy cryptography should not be silently selected.

**Important distinction:** CRC and non-cryptographic hashes are useful for checksums, indexing, and error detection, but are not substitutes for secure cryptographic integrity or authentication.

### **Ownership rule**

`Robix.Collections` should own the canonical implementations of general-purpose collections such as `List<T>`, `HashMap<K,V>`, and `Queue<T>`.

`Robix.Algorithms.DataStructures` should expose specialized structures and their algorithms without creating duplicate, incompatible implementations of the same collection.

**Concurrency correctness requirements:** Parallel operations must specify whether their callbacks need to be thread-safe, whether their output order is preserved, and whether results are deterministic. Floating-point reductions must document that different reduction orders can produce different rounding results.

# **30. Shared interfaces for all 29 packages**

The algorithms ecosystem should use a common set of interfaces rather than defining incompatible abstractions inside every package.

| Interface                             | Responsibility                    |
| ------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| `IAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>`          | General algorithm execution       |
| `IAsyncAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>`     | Asynchronous algorithm execution  |
| `IParallelAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>`  | Parallel algorithm execution      |
| `IStreamingAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>` | Streaming computation             |
| `ICancellableAlgorithm`               | Cancellation support              |
| `IProgressReportingAlgorithm`         | Progress notifications            |
| `ISorter<T>`                          | Sorting contract                  |
| `ISearcher<T>`                        | Searching contract                |
| `ISelector<T>`                        | Selection contract                |
| `IComparer<T>`                        | Value comparison                  |
| `IEqualityComparer<T>`                | Equality and hashing              |
| `IHasher<T>`                          | Hashing operations                |
| `IEncoder<TInput,TOutput>`            | Encoding contract                 |
| `IDecoder<TInput,TOutput>`            | Decoding contract                 |
| `ICompressor`                         | Compression contract              |
| `IDecompressor`                       | Decompression contract            |
| `IGraph<TVertex,TEdge>`               | Graph abstraction                 |
| `ITree<T>`                            | Tree abstraction                  |
| `IHeuristic<T>`                       | Heuristic evaluation              |
| `IOptimizationProblem<T>`             | Optimization problem definition   |
| `IOptimizer<T>`                       | Optimization contract             |
| `IClusteringAlgorithm<T>`             | Clustering contract               |
| `IClassifier<TInput,TLabel>`          | Classification contract           |
| `IRegressor<TInput,TOutput>`          | Regression contract               |
| `IProbabilityDistribution<T>`         | Probability distribution contract |
| `IRandomGenerator`                    | Reproducible randomness           |
| `IDistanceMetric<T>`                  | Distance calculation              |
| `ISimilarityMetric<T>`                | Similarity calculation            |
| `IDataStructure<T>`                   | Common data-structure operations  |

## **Example of a type-safe algorithm interface**

```
Package Robix.Algorithms;


Public Interface IAlgorithm<TInput, TOutput> {

    Public Function Execute(
        TInput input,
        AlgorithmOptions options
    ) -> Result<TOutput, AlgorithmError>;

    Public Async Function ExecuteAsync(
        TInput input,
        AlgorithmOptions options,
        CancellationToken cancellation
    ) -> Task<Result<TOutput, AlgorithmError>>;

    Public Function GetMetadata() -> AlgorithmMetadata;
}
```

An implementation could look like:

```
Package Robix.Algorithms.Sorting;


Import Robix.Algorithms.*;
Import Robix.Collections.*;

Public Class MergeSorter<T>
    Implements IAlgorithm<Array<T>, Array<T>>
    Where T: IComparable<T> {

    Public Function Execute(
        Array<T> input,
        AlgorithmOptions options
    ) -> Result<Array<T>, AlgorithmError> {

        Array<T> result = input.Clone();
        MergeSort(result);

        Return Success(result);
    }

    Public Async Function ExecuteAsync(
        Array<T> input,
        AlgorithmOptions options,
        CancellationToken cancellation
    ) -> Task<Result<Array<T>, AlgorithmError>> {

        cancellation.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();

        Return Await Task.Run(
            Function() => Execute(input, options),
            cancellation
        );
    }

    Public Function GetMetadata() -> AlgorithmMetadata {
        Return AlgorithmMetadata {
            Name: "Merge Sort",
            TimeComplexity: "O(n log n)",
            SpaceComplexity: "O(n)",
            Stable: True,
            InPlace: False,
            Deterministic: True
        };
    }
}
```

This is illustrative Robix syntax. The exact interface, `Explain` documentation format, and task APIs should be aligned with the final compiler grammar.

# **31. Algorithm metadata requirements**

Every public algorithm implementation should provide metadata covering:

| Property                | Meaning                                                                           |
| ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Name`                  | Algorithm name                                                                    |
| `Category`              | Package or algorithm family                                                       |
| `Version`               | Implementation version                                                            |
| `TimeComplexityBest`    | Best-case time complexity                                                         |
| `TimeComplexityAverage` | Average-case time complexity                                                      |
| `TimeComplexityWorst`   | Worst-case time complexity                                                        |
| `SpaceComplexity`       | Auxiliary-space requirements                                                      |
| `Stable`                | Whether ordering of equivalent elements is preserved                              |
| `InPlace`               | Whether the implementation mutates its input                                      |
| `Deterministic`         | Whether equivalent inputs produce equivalent results under the stated environment |
| `Parallelizable`        | Whether parallel execution is supported                                           |
| `ThreadSafe`            | Whether simultaneous use is safe                                                  |
| `StreamingSupported`    | Whether streaming input is supported                                              |
| `Cancelable`            | Whether cancellation is supported                                                 |
| `InputRequirements`     | Preconditions and supported data types                                            |
| `OutputGuarantees`      | Guarantees of successful execution                                                |
| `ErrorTypes`            | Possible errors                                                                   |
| `PlatformRequirements`  | Required CPU, GPU, or other capabilities                                          |
| `PrecisionRequirements` | Numerical precision assumptions                                                   |

## **32. Recommended general-purpose algorithm functions**

For the convenience of developers, the library should expose a small, canonical high-level API while keeping specialized algorithms in their respective packages.

```
Import Robix.Algorithms.*;
Import Robix.Algorithms.Sorting As Sorting;
Import Robix.Algorithms.Searching As Searching;
Import Robix.Algorithms.Graphs As Graphs;
Import Robix.Algorithms.DataStructures As Structures;
```

Developers could then write:

```

Array<Int> numbers = [9, 4, 7, 1, 5, 2];

// Sort the array.
Sorting.Sort(numbers);

// Search a sorted array.
Option<Int> index = Searching.BinarySearch(numbers, 5);

// Find the three largest values.
Array<Int> largest = Selection.TopK(numbers, 3);

// Create and use a graph.
Graph<Int, Float> graph = Graph<Int, Float>();

graph.AddVertex(1);
graph.AddVertex(2);
graph.AddEdge(1, 2, 5.0);

GraphPath<Int> path = Graphs.ShortestPath(graph, 1, 2);

// Create a heap.
MinHeap<Int> heap = MinHeap<Int>();

heap.Insert(10);
heap.Insert(3);
heap.Insert(8);

Int smallest = heap.ExtractMin();
```

# **33. Required rules for the Robix algorithms library**

I recommend making these rules compulsory for official algorithm implementations.

1. **Strict type safety:** Every package must compile under strict typing.
2. **Generic support:** Algorithms should work with arbitrary compatible numeric and comparable types.
3. **Explicit mutation:** APIs should distinguish in-place operations from operations returning new collections.
4. **Deterministic behavior:** Algorithms should document their behavior and accept explicit seeds when using randomness.
5. **Complexity documentation:** Public algorithms must document time and space complexity, including important preconditions.
6. **Error handling:** Recoverable failures should use typed errors or `Result<T,E>`.
7. **Async support:** Long-running or I/O-bound algorithms should support asynchronous execution where useful.
8. **Cancellation:** Expensive computations should support `CancellationToken`.
9. **Thread safety:** Parallel algorithms must document shared-state and callback requirements.
10. **Memory safety:** Algorithms must respect collection ownership, bounds checking, and safe resource management.
11. **No redundant implementations:** Shared foundational algorithms should have one canonical implementation.
12. **Stable interfaces:** Implementations may improve without unnecessarily breaking public API compatibility.
13. **Secure implementations:** Cryptographic functions must rely on maintained and reviewed implementations.
14. **Input validation:** Algorithms must reject invalid parameters and unsupported input configurations clearly.
15. **Platform detection:** GPU and hardware-accelerated algorithms must check capabilities before execution.


## 4. Text, strings, and data formats

# **ROBIX — Text, Strings, and Data Formats API Specification**

21 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Encoders · Parsers · Serializers · Security · Async APIs

This is the proposed **Robix Text and Data Standard Library (RTDSL)**, following the same approach as the previous Core, Mathematics, and Algorithms specifications.

It covers all 21 packages you provided, including their classes, types, interfaces, functions, advanced features, and recommended implementation rules.

The API design should satisfy these requirements:

- **Mandatory type safety:** Every official implementation must support strict typing.
- **Synchronous and asynchronous APIs:** Especially for files, streams, large documents, compression, and archives.
- **Full Unicode support:** Including UTF-8, UTF-16, UTF-32, grapheme clusters, normalization, and international text.
- **Streaming:** Large files should not need to be loaded entirely into memory.
- **Error handling:** Typed errors through `Result<T, E>`, with exceptions where appropriate.
- **Security:** Safe parsing, bounded resource consumption, and protection against malicious input.
- **Interoperability:** Support recognized format and protocol standards.

These are proposed Robix interfaces, not claims about an already implemented library. This specification aims for comprehensive coverage, but final API completeness will depend on the supported standards, platforms, and Robix runtime.

```

**Syntax trees**

```

```

```
Deserialize<T>(data)
Deserialize<T>(data, options)
TryDeserialize<T>(data)
DeserializeFromBytes<T>(bytes, format)
DeserializeFromStream<T>(stream, format)
DeserializeFromFile<T>(path, format)
DeserializeAsync<T>(stream, format)
DeserializeFromFileAsync<T>(path, format)
```

**Contracts and schemas**

```
RegisterContract<T>(contract)
GetContract<T>()
ValidateContract<T>(value)
GenerateSchema<T>(format)
ValidateSchema(data, schema)
ResolveSchemaReference(reference)
```

**Converters**

```
RegisterConverter<T>(converter)
UnregisterConverter<T>()
FindConverter<T>()
ConvertSerializedValue<T>(value)
```

**Type handling**

```
RegisterType<T>()
ResolveType(identifier)
GetSerializationTypeInfo<T>()
SetPolymorphicTypePolicy(policy)
ValidatePolymorphicType(type)
```

**Versioning**

```
GetSchemaVersion(data)
RegisterMigration(fromVersion, toVersion, migration)
MigrateSerializedData(data, targetVersion)
CheckSchemaCompatibility(oldSchema, newSchema)
```

**References and cyclic objects**

```
EnableReferenceTracking(options)
ResolveObjectReference(id)
RegisterObjectReference(object)
DetectReferenceCycle(object)
```

**Validation and diagnostics**

```
ValidateSerializedData(data, format)
InspectSerializedData(data)
GetSerializationDiagnostics()
GetSerializedSize(value, format)
BenchmarkSerializer(serializer, dataset)
```

**Critical security rules:** Deserializers must not construct arbitrary types supplied by untrusted data, execute constructors with unintended side effects, or access private resources without explicit authorization. Supported polymorphic types should be registered or allowlisted.

**Security:** Extraction must prevent path traversal, absolute-path escape, unsafe symbolic links, decompression bombs, and uncontrolled file overwrites. Not every archive format supports writing or encryption; availability must be reported accurately.

**Security:** MIME types inferred from extensions or content signatures are advisory. They must not be treated as proof that a file is safe. Multipart parsing needs strict boundary, header-size, part-count, and total-body limits.

# **22. Shared interfaces for all 21 packages**

To ensure every package works with the others, Robix should define common interfaces rather than introducing incompatible readers, writers, serializers, and encoders.

| Interface                  | Responsibility           |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------ |
| `ITextReader`              | Sequential text reading  |
| `ITextWriter`              | Sequential text writing  |
| `IStringConvertible<T>`    | String conversion        |
| `IStringFormattable`       | Custom string formatting |
| `ITextParser<T>`           | Typed text parsing       |
| `ITextFormatter<T>`        | Typed text formatting    |
| `IEncoder<TInput,TOutput>` | Encoding                 |
| `IDecoder<TInput,TOutput>` | Decoding                 |
| `IIncrementalEncoder`      | Incremental encoding     |
| `IIncrementalDecoder`      | Incremental decoding     |
| `IParser<T>`               | General parsing          |
| `IStreamingParser<T>`      | Streaming parsing        |
| `ISerializer<T>`           | Object serialization     |
| `IDeserializer<T>`         | Object deserialization   |
| `ISchemaValidator<T>`      | Schema validation        |
| `ICompressor`              | Compression              |
| `IDecompressor`            | Decompression            |
| `IArchiveReader`           | Archive reading          |
| `IArchiveWriter`           | Archive writing          |
| `IBinaryReader`            | Binary reading           |
| `IBinaryWriter`            | Binary writing           |
| `IBuffer<T>`               | Buffer access            |
| `IBufferWriter<T>`         | Buffer output            |
| `ICodec<TInput,TOutput>`   | General codec            |
| `IMIMEProvider`            | Media-type lookup        |
| `IAsyncDisposable`         | Async resource disposal  |

## **23. Required API conventions**

| Concern            | Required convention                                           |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Type safety        | strict typing for compiled code                         |
| Classes            | PascalCase                                                    |
| Acronyms           | `JSON`, `XML`, `YAML`, `TOML`, `CSV`, `MIME`, `UTF8`, `ASCII` |
| Async APIs         | `Async` suffix and `Task<T>` return types                     |
| Fallible parsing   | `TryParse` or `Result<T, E>`                                  |
| Immutable strings  | `String` should be immutable                                  |
| Mutable text       | Use `StringBuilder` or `MutableString`                        |
| Large files        | Support stream-based processing                               |
| Character encoding | UTF-8 default, explicit alternatives                          |
| Memory management  | Explicit buffer ownership and disposal                        |
| Resource limits    | Configurable size, depth, and time limits                     |
| Serialization      | Explicit type contracts                                       |
| Format compliance  | Document supported standard versions                          |
| Security           | Safe-by-default parsing and deserialization                   |

## **24. Recommended error hierarchy**

```
TextError
    StringError
    UnicodeError
    EncodingError
    FormattingError
    ParsingError
    RegexError
    TemplateError

DataFormatError
    JSONError
    XMLError
    YAMLError
    TOMLError
    CSVError
    MIMEError

SerializationError
    SchemaValidationError
    UnsupportedTypeError
    CircularReferenceError
    UnsafeDeserializationError

BinaryError
    BufferBoundsError
    InvalidBinaryLayoutError
    EndiannessError

CompressionError
    UnsupportedCompressionError
    CorruptCompressedDataError
    DecompressionLimitError

ArchiveError
    CorruptArchiveError
    UnsupportedArchiveFormatError
    UnsafeArchivePathError

CodecError
    UnsupportedCodecError
    InvalidEncodedDataError
```

All these error types should have the common fields `Code`, `Message`, `Details`, and optional `InnerError`.

## **25. Example Robix application**

The following illustrates how these libraries could be used together.

```
Package Robotics.DataProcessor;


Import Robix.String As RoboString;
Import Robix.Text As Text;
Import Robix.JSON As JSON;
Import Robix.CSV As CSV;
Import Robix.XML As XML;
Import Robix.Compression As Compression;
Import Robix.Archives As Archives;
Import Robix.Text.Encoding As Encoding;
Import Robix.Text.RegularExpressions As Regex;
Import Robix.IO.Files As Files;

Public Async Function ProcessData()
    -> Task<Result<Void, DataFormatError>>
Explain {
    Description: "Process and export robot data.";
    Input: None;
    Processing: "Read, parse, filter, serialize, and compress data.";
    Output: "Success or a typed data processing error.";
} {
    Try {
        // Read file asynchronously.
        String input = Await Files.ReadTextAsync("robots.json");

        // Parse JSON into a typed collection.
        Array<RobotData> robots =
            JSON.Deserialize<Array<RobotData>>(input);

        // Filter the robots.
        Array<RobotData> active =
            robots.Filter(Function(robot) => robot.Active);

        // Convert records to CSV.
        String csvText = CSV.Serialize(active);

        // Write the results.
        Await Files.WriteTextAsync("active.csv", csvText);

        // Compress the output.
        Array<Byte> compressed =
            Compression.Gzip(
                Encoding.EncodeUTF8(csvText)
            );

        Await Files.WriteBytesAsync(
            "active.csv.gz",
            compressed
        );

        Return Success();
    }
    Catch (DataFormatError error) {
        Return Failure(error);
    }
}
```

## **26. Recommended package ownership and dependencies**

| Package                         | Main dependencies                                      |
| ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Robix.String`                  | `Robix.Core`, `Robix.Text.Unicode`                     |
| `Robix.Text`                    | `Robix.String`                                         |
| `Robix.Text.Encoding`           | `Robix.Buffers`, `Robix.Text.Unicode`                  |
| `Robix.Text.Unicode`            | `Robix.Core`                                           |
| `Robix.Text.RegularExpressions` | `Robix.String`, `Robix.Text`                           |
| `Robix.Text.Parsing`            | `Robix.Text`                                           |
| `Robix.Text.Formatting`         | `Robix.String`, `Robix.Globalization`                  |
| `Robix.Text.Markdown`           | `Robix.Text.Parsing`                                   |
| `Robix.Text.Templates`          | `Robix.Text.Parsing`, `Robix.Text.Formatting`          |
| `Robix.JSON`                    | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.XML`                     | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.YAML`                    | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.TOML`                    | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.CSV`                     | `Robix.Text`, `Robix.Text.Encoding`                    |
| `Robix.Serialization`           | `Robix.Core`, serialization contracts                  |
| `Robix.Compression`             | `Robix.Buffers`, algorithm backends                    |
| `Robix.Archives`                | `Robix.IO`, `Robix.Compression`                        |
| `Robix.Binary`                  | `Robix.Buffers`, `Robix.Numerics`                      |
| `Robix.Buffers`                 | `Robix.Memory`, `Robix.Core`                           |
| `Robix.Codecs`                  | Codec interfaces, individual format providers          |
| `Robix.MIME`                    | `Robix.String`, `Robix.Text.Parsing`                   |

These are logical relationships rather than required direct dependencies. In particular, `Robix.Serialization` should define contracts independently enough that format packages can implement those contracts without circular dependencies.

## **27. Important design decisions**

Before implementation, these decisions should be finalized:

1. Whether `String` indexing refers to Unicode code points, code units, bytes, or grapheme clusters.
2. Whether string slices borrow the original string's storage or copy their contents.
3. Which Unicode version Robix supports.
4. Whether format parsers preserve comments and source formatting where those features are permitted.
5. Whether JSON numeric values retain arbitrary precision.
6. Which YAML and TOML specification versions are supported.
7. Whether serializers require compile-time generated metadata or permit reflection.
8. Which regular-expression features are supported by each execution engine.
9. Whether all streaming APIs follow a common backpressure contract.
10. Which archive formats support reading, writing, encryption, and streaming.
11. How pooled buffers prevent use-after-release.
12. Which codecs ship by default and which require optional native providers.

### **Final recommendation**

I recommend treating `Robix.String`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Text.Unicode`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Text.Encoding`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Buffers`**, and&#x20;**`Robix.Binary`**&#x20;as foundational layers**.

Build the parsers and format libraries on top of these foundations, with shared serialization and streaming contracts. That makes it possible to support additional formats such as CBOR, MessagePack, Protocol Buffers, Apache Avro, and Apache Parquet later without redesigning the standard library.

## 5. Filesystem, I/O, and storage

# **ROBIX — Filesystem, I/O, and Storage API Specification**

17 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Async APIs · Storage providers · Transactions · Security

This specification covers all **17 Filesystem, I/O, and Storage packages** you listed, following the same approach as the previous Robix API catalogues.

Each package includes its proposed classes, structures, interfaces, functions, and advanced capabilities.

The API design should follow these principles:

- **Mandatory type safety:** Every official package must support strict typing during compilation.
- **Synchronous and asynchronous operations:** File, network-backed storage, database, and stream operations should provide async alternatives.
- **Platform independence:** Portable APIs must work across Windows, Linux, and macOS wherever possible.
- **Resource safety:** Files, streams, database connections, and transactions must support deterministic disposal.
- **Error handling:** Recoverable failures should use typed errors such as `Result<T, IOError>`.
- **Concurrency:** Support cancellation tokens, file locking, atomic operations, and safe concurrent access.
- **Performance:** Support buffering, pooling, memory mapping, zero-copy operations, and streaming large files.
- **Security:** Include permission checking, path validation, secure temporary files, and protection against resource exhaustion.

These APIs are proposed for Robix; they are not claims of existing implementation. The catalogue aims for comprehensive functional coverage, though a literally final API requires agreed runtime, storage, and platform contracts.

## **5.&#x20;**`Robix.IO.Buffers`

```

This package should reuse the canonical buffer types and ownership rules from `Robix.Buffers`. It should specialize in how those buffers interact with I/O rather than introducing incompatible versions of the same abstractions.

**Backpressure**

```

Pipelines.ParseStreamAsync(source, parser)
```

**Required design:** Backpressure is compulsory for bounded pipelines. Cancellation, errors, and completion must propagate predictably through all stages, and disposable resources must be released when processing terminates.

**Security:** Lexical path normalization is not equivalent to secure filesystem resolution. Protection against symlink traversal and time-of-check/time-of-use races requires filesystem-aware operations, directory handles, and suitable platform facilities.

- `DistributedCache<TKey, TValue>`
- `FileCache<TKey, TValue>`
- `DatabaseCache<TKey, TValue>`
- `HybridCache<TKey, TValue>`
- `MultiLevelCache<TKey, TValue>`
- `ReadThroughCache<TKey, TValue>`
- `WriteThroughCache<TKey, TValue>`
- `WriteBehindCache<TKey, TValue>`

**Eviction**

- `CacheEvictionPolicy`
- `LRUEvictionPolicy`
- `LFUEvictionPolicy`
- `FIFOE victionPolicy`
- `RandomEvictionPolicy`
- `TTLEvictionPolicy`
- `SizeBasedEvictionPolicy`
- `PriorityEvictionPolicy`

**Advanced**

- `CacheLock`
- `CacheLease`
- `CacheTag`
- `CacheDependency`
- `CacheInvalidation`
- `CacheStampedeProtection`
- `CacheSerializer<T>`
- `CacheCompressionOptions`
- `CacheConsistencyPolicy`
- `CacheRefreshPolicy`
- `CacheLoader<TKey, TValue>`
- `CacheWriter<TKey, TValue>`

Canonical spelling of the FIFO class should be `FIFOEvictionPolicy`; the spaced spelling above is not an API identifier.

### **Functions**

**Creation**

```
Cache.Create<TKey, TValue>()

Cache.CreateMemoryCache<TKey, TValue>()
Cache.CreateDistributedCache<TKey, TValue>(provider)
Cache.CreateHybridCache<TKey, TValue>(providers)
```

**Reading**

```

cache.GetAsync(key)

cache.GetOrAdd(key, factory)
cache.GetOrAddAsync(key, factory)
cache.GetMany(keys)
cache.GetManyAsync(keys)
cache.Contains(key)
```

**Writing**

```

cache.SetMany(entries)
```

**Removal and invalidation**

```

cache.ClearAsync()

```

**Expiration**

```

```

**Eviction**

```

```

**Advanced**

```

```

**Statistics**

```

cache.GetMissCount()
cache.GetHitRate()
cache.GetEvictionCount()
cache.GetEntryCount()
cache.GetMemoryUsage()
cache.GetStatistics()
cache.ResetStatistics()
```

**Reliability:** Caches must be treated as potentially unavailable or stale unless an application explicitly establishes stronger guarantees. Cache eviction must not cause permanent data loss.

**Graph operations**

```

```

**Transactions**

NoSQL.GetHealth()
NoSQL.Ping()
```

**Important:** NoSQL is a family of different database models, not a single universal query language. Document, key-value, graph, and wide-column APIs should expose separate, strongly typed capabilities. Transactions, joins, aggregations, and consistency guarantees vary by provider.

# **18. Shared interfaces for all 17 packages**

Every package should reuse common contracts wherever possible.

| Interface                        | Responsibility                  |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| `IReadable`                      | Read operations                 |
| `IWritable`                      | Write operations                |
| `ISeekable`                      | Position-based operations       |
| `IStream`                        | General stream abstraction      |
| `IAsyncStream`                   | Asynchronous stream abstraction |
| `IFileSystem`                    | Filesystem operations           |
| `IFile`                          | File abstraction                |
| `IDirectory`                     | Directory abstraction           |
| `IFileWatcher`                   | File change notifications       |
| `IBuffer<T>`                     | Buffer operations               |
| `IBufferPool<T>`                 | Pooled memory                   |
| `IPipelineStage<TInput,TOutput>` | Pipeline processing             |
| `IStorage`                       | General storage abstraction     |
| `IStorageProvider`               | Storage provider                |
| `ITransactionalStorage`          | Transactional storage           |
| `IVersionedStorage`              | Version-aware storage           |
| `IStorageSerializer<T>`          | Typed storage serialization     |
| `ICache<TKey,TValue>`            | Cache operations                |
| `IKeyValueStore<TKey,TValue>`    | Key-value storage               |
| `IConfiguration`                 | Configuration operations        |
| `IConfigurationProvider`         | Configuration source            |
| `IDatabaseProvider`              | Database provider               |
| `IDatabaseConnection`            | Database connection             |
| `IDatabaseTransaction`           | Database transaction            |
| `IDatabaseQuery<T>`              | Typed database querying         |
| `IDisposable`                    | Deterministic resource disposal |
| `IAsyncDisposable`               | Asynchronous disposal           |

## **19. Required error hierarchy**

I recommend the following shared error categories:

```
IOError
    FileNotFoundError
    DirectoryNotFoundError
    PermissionDeniedError
    FileAlreadyExistsError
    PathTooLongError
    InvalidPathError
    EndOfStreamError
    StreamClosedError
    DeviceUnavailableError
    IOErrorTimeout
    IOCancelledError

StorageError
    StorageUnavailableError
    StorageNotFoundError
    StorageConflictError
    StorageQuotaExceededError
    StorageReadOnlyError
    StorageCorruptionError
    StorageTransactionError
    StorageVersionMismatchError

CacheError
    CacheUnavailableError
    CacheSerializationError
    CacheCapacityError

ConfigurationError
    ConfigurationNotFoundError
    ConfigurationValidationError
    ConfigurationBindingError
    ConfigurationProviderError

DatabaseError
    DatabaseConnectionError
    DatabaseQueryError
    DatabaseTimeoutError
    DatabaseConstraintError
    DatabaseTransactionError
    DatabaseAuthenticationError
    DatabaseAuthorizationError
    DatabaseSchemaError
    DatabaseMigrationError
    DatabaseProviderError
```

Every error should expose a stable error code, message, optional underlying error, and contextual metadata. Sensitive information such as passwords or database credentials must never appear in diagnostic messages.

# **20. Common API conventions**

| Requirement         | Convention                                                               |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Type safety         | strict typing mandatory for compiled modules                       |
| Naming              | PascalCase for public types and functions                                |
| Acronyms            | Uppercase `IO`, `SQL`, `JSON`, `XML`, `YAML`, `TOML`, `TCP`, `TLS`, etc. |
| Async methods       | `Async` suffix and `Task<T>` return type                                 |
| Fallible operations | `Try` variants or `Result<T,E>`                                          |
| Cancellation        | `CancellationToken`                                                      |
| Resource cleanup    | `IDisposable` or `IAsyncDisposable`                                      |
| Large data          | Streaming and bounded buffering                                          |
| File paths          | `Robix.Paths` abstractions                                               |
| Queries             | Parameterized or typed query representations                             |
| Transactions        | Explicit commit and rollback                                             |
| Persistence         | Clearly documented durability guarantees                                 |
| Concurrency         | Document isolation and thread safety                                     |
| Security            | Least-privilege access and validated inputs                              |

## **21. Example: Type-safe asynchronous file processing**

```
Package Robotics.DataStorage;


Import Robix.IO.Files As Files;
Import Robix.IO.Streams As Streams;
Import Robix.Paths As Paths;
Import Robix.Storage.Local As Local;
Import Robix.Storage.Cache As Cache;
Import Robix.JSON As JSON;

Public Async Function SaveRobotData(
    String robotId,
    RobotData data,
    CancellationToken cancellation
) -> Task<Result<Void, StorageError>>
Explain {
    Description: "Store robot data in local storage.";
    Input:
        robotId = "Unique robot identifier",
        data = "Robot data to save",
        cancellation = "Cancellation token";
    Processing: "Serialize and save the data asynchronously.";
    Output: "Success or a typed storage error.";
} {
    Try {
        String filename = robotId + ".json";
        String path = Paths.Join("robots", filename);

        String json = JSON.Serialize(data);

        Await Files.WriteTextAsync(
            path,
            json,
            cancellation
        );

        Return Success();
    }
    Catch (StorageError error) {
        Return Failure(error);
    }
}
```

For a fully type-safe implementation, file-related errors such as `IOError` should be mapped explicitly into the declared `StorageError` return type, rather than relying on implicit conversion.

## **22. Recommended package ownership**

Avoid creating multiple incompatible implementations of the same functionality.

| Canonical owner               | What it owns                            |
| ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| `Robix.IO`                    | General reader/writer and I/O contracts |
| `Robix.IO.Files`              | File operations                         |
| `Robix.IO.Directories`        | Directory operations                    |
| `Robix.IO.Streams`            | Stream implementations                  |
| `Robix.IO.Buffers`            | I/O-specific buffer integration         |
| `Robix.IO.Pipelines`          | Pipeline processing                     |
| `Robix.Paths`                 | Path operations                         |
| `Robix.Storage`               | Generic storage contracts               |
| `Robix.Storage.Memory`        | In-memory storage providers             |
| `Robix.Storage.Local`         | Local persistent storage                |
| `Robix.Storage.Database`      | Storage API backed by databases         |
| `Robix.Storage.Cache`         | Caching                                 |
| `Robix.Storage.KeyValue`      | Key-value storage                       |
| `Robix.Storage.Configuration` | Configuration providers                 |
| `Robix.Databases`             | Database connection and query contracts |
| `Robix.Databases.SQL`         | Relational database APIs                |
| `Robix.Databases.NoSQL`       | Non-relational database APIs            |

### **Final architecture recommendation**

Robix should treat `Robix.IO`**,&#x20;**`Robix.IO.Streams`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Paths`**, and&#x20;**`Robix.Storage`**&#x20;as the foundational abstractions** for this category.

Filesystem packages should build on the I/O and path foundations. Storage providers should implement `Robix.Storage` contracts. Database-backed storage should reuse database providers, avoiding duplicate implementations.


## 6. Networking and communication

# **ROBIX — Networking and Communication API Specification**

21 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Async APIs · Protocols · Security · Networking infrastructure

This is the proposed **Robix Networking and Communication Standard Library (RNCSL)**, covering all 21 packages you listed.

The specification is designed for client and server applications, cloud systems, distributed computing, network automation, robotics, industrial devices, and embedded applications.

Every package should follow these common requirements:

- **Strict type safety:** Official APIs must support strict typing.
- **Asynchronous I/O:** Network operations should support `Async`, `Await`, `Task<T>`, and `CancellationToken`.
- **Secure defaults:** TLS certificate validation, protected credentials, and bounded network operations.
- **Cross-platform behavior:** Explicitly report unsupported operating-system or hardware features.
- **Typed errors:** Distinguish transport, protocol, authentication, timeout, cancellation, and validation failures.
- **Resource safety:** Connections, sockets, channels, and streams support deterministic disposal.
- **Extensibility:** Shared interfaces should allow custom transports and protocol implementations.

This is a proposed API design, not a claim that these packages are already implemented. The function inventories are intended as a comprehensive implementation baseline.

TCP.ReceiveAsync(connection, buffer)
TCP.ReceiveExactly(connection, count)
TCP.ReceiveExactlyAsync(connection, count)
TCP.CopyToStreamAsync(connection, destination)
TCP.CopyFromStreamAsync(connection, source)
```

**Stream access**

```
TCP.GetStream(connection)
TCP.GetReader(connection)
TCP.GetWriter(connection)
TCP.FlushAsync(connection)
TCP.ShutdownRead(connection)
TCP.ShutdownWrite(connection)
TCP.ShutdownBoth(connection)
```

**Configuration**

```
TCP.SetNoDelay(connection, enabled)
TCP.GetNoDelay(connection)
TCP.SetKeepAlive(connection, options)
TCP.SetReceiveBufferSize(connection, size)
TCP.SetSendBufferSize(connection, size)
TCP.SetReceiveTimeout(connection, timeout)
TCP.SetSendTimeout(connection, timeout)
TCP.SetLinger(connection, options)
```

**Monitoring**

```
TCP.GetLocalEndpoint(connection)
TCP.GetRemoteEndpoint(connection)
TCP.GetBytesSent(connection)
TCP.GetBytesReceived(connection)
TCP.GetStatistics(connection)
TCP.GetConnectionDuration(connection)
```

**Important:** TCP is a byte stream, not a message protocol. `Send` and `Receive` must not imply message boundaries. Applications needing messages must use framing or a higher-level protocol.

## **6.&#x20;**`Robix.Network.UDP`

**Responsibility:** Connectionless datagram transport, packet sending and receiving, multicast support, and datagram-oriented communication.

### **Classes and types**

- `UDPStatistics`

- `UDPPacketTooLargeError`

### **Functions**

```

UDP.GetStatistics(socket)

```

**Important:** UDP does not guarantee delivery, ordering, or duplicate suppression. Reliable delivery and retransmission must be supplied by an explicitly selected higher-level protocol.

**Security:** Plain FTP transmits credentials and data without transport encryption. The standard library should discourage it on untrusted networks and direct users toward SFTP or an authenticated TLS-protected transfer method.

```

SFTP is not FTP over TLS. It is a separate file-transfer protocol carried over SSH. Server extensions, atomic renames, and remote copy operations must be capability-gated.

```

```

```

**Certificates**

```

**Security configuration**

```

```

```

```

**Advanced**

```

TLS.RequestKeyUpdate(connection)
TLS.GetChannelBinding(connection, bindingType)
TLS.GetPeerCertificateChain(connection)
```

**Required security defaults:** Prefer TLS 1.3 with securely configured TLS 1.2 compatibility where needed. Certificate-chain and hostname validation must not be disabled implicitly. TLS libraries should handle nonce generation, key schedules, and handshake authentication internally.

```

**Safety:** Hardware control APIs must not assume that receiving a frame proves its authenticity. CAN traffic can be safety-critical, so diagnostics, permissions, rate limits, and device-specific safeguards should be built into the API.

**Important:** QUIC uses TLS 1.3 for its security handshake but is not simply TLS layered over TCP. QUIC operates over UDP and has its own transport security integration, streams, congestion control, and connection identifiers. Zero-RTT application data requires explicit replay-risk handling.

# **22. Shared interfaces for all 21 networking packages**

Robix should define common interfaces so that transport protocols, secure connections, client/server libraries, and messaging systems can interoperate.

| Interface                 | Responsibility                    |
| ------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| `INetworkTransport`       | Generic network transport         |
| `IConnection`             | Connection lifecycle              |
| `IAsyncConnection`        | Asynchronous connection lifecycle |
| `IConnectionFactory`      | Connection creation               |
| `IConnectionPool`         | Connection pooling                |
| `IListener`               | Incoming connection handling      |
| `IEndpoint`               | Endpoint abstraction              |
| `IAddressResolver`        | Address resolution                |
| `IStreamTransport`        | Byte-stream transport             |
| `IDatagramTransport`      | Datagram transport                |
| `ISecureTransport`        | Secure transport                  |
| `ICertificateValidator`   | Certificate validation            |
| `IAuthenticationProvider` | Authentication                    |
| `IProxyConnector`         | Proxy connections                 |
| `IHTTPRequestHandler`     | HTTP request handling             |
| `IHTTPMiddleware`         | HTTP middleware                   |
| `IWebSocketHandler`       | WebSocket messages                |
| `IMessagePublisher<T>`    | Message publishing                |
| `IMessageSubscriber<T>`   | Message subscription              |
| `IMessageSerializer<T>`   | Message serialization             |
| `IRPCClient`              | RPC client operations             |
| `IRPCService`             | RPC service implementations       |
| `IDiscoveryProvider`      | Service discovery                 |
| `IMulticastTransport`     | Multicast transport               |
| `ICANController`          | CAN hardware abstraction          |
| `IQUICConnection`         | QUIC connection operations        |
| `INetworkDiagnostics`     | Network diagnostics               |
| `INetworkStatistics`      | Network statistics                |

## **23. Recommended error hierarchy**

```
NetworkError
    NetworkUnavailableError
    NetworkTimeoutError
    NetworkPermissionError
    NetworkAddressError
    NetworkResolutionError
    NetworkConnectionError
    NetworkDisconnectedError
    NetworkProtocolError
    NetworkAuthenticationError
    NetworkCancellationError

HTTPError
    HTTPRequestError
    HTTPResponseError
    HTTPProtocolError
    HTTPTimeoutError
    HTTPRedirectError

TLSError
    TLSHandshakeError
    TLSCertificateError
    TLSAuthenticationError
    TLSProtocolError

SSHError
    SSHAuthenticationError
    SSHHostKeyError
    SSHConnectionError
    SSHChannelError

DNSError
    DNSResolutionError
    DNSResponseError
    DNSTimeoutError
    DNSSECValidationError

FTPError
    FTPAuthenticationError
    FTPTransferError
    FTPProtocolError

SFTPError
    SFTPAuthenticationError
    SFTPFileError
    SFTPTransferError

WebSocketError
    WebSocketHandshakeError
    WebSocketProtocolError
    WebSocketClosedError

MessagingError
    MessageDeliveryError
    MessageSerializationError
    MessageAcknowledgementError

RPCError
    RPCTransportError
    RPCDeadlineExceededError
    RPCServiceUnavailableError

CANError
    CANBusError
    CANControllerError
    CANFrameError

QUICError
    QUICTransportError
    QUICStreamError
    QUICHandshakeError
    QUICApplicationError
```

Each error should expose `Code`, `Message`, `Details`, optional `InnerError`, and an indication of whether retrying is potentially appropriate.

# **24. Shared networking configuration types**

| Type                           | Purpose                                   |
| ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- |
| `NetworkTimeoutOptions`        | Connect, read, write, and total deadlines |
| `NetworkRetryPolicy`           | Retry behavior and backoff                |
| `NetworkCancellationOptions`   | Cancellation behavior                     |
| `NetworkBufferOptions`         | Buffer sizes and ownership                |
| `NetworkRateLimitOptions`      | Bandwidth and request limits              |
| `NetworkSecurityPolicy`        | Security requirements                     |
| `NetworkAuthenticationOptions` | Authentication configuration              |
| `NetworkProxyOptions`          | Proxy configuration                       |
| `NetworkLoggingOptions`        | Logging and redaction                     |
| `NetworkTracingOptions`        | Distributed tracing                       |
| `NetworkMetricsOptions`        | Connection and transport metrics          |
| `NetworkKeepAliveOptions`      | Idle connection handling                  |
| `NetworkConnectionPoolOptions` | Connection reuse                          |
| `NetworkBackpressureOptions`   | Bounded pending-data policies             |

## **25. Common networking API conventions**

| Requirement      | Rule                                                                                             |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Type safety      | All official APIs must support strict typing                                               |
| Classes          | PascalCase                                                                                       |
| Acronyms         | Preserve `HTTP`, `HTTPS`, `TCP`, `UDP`, `DNS`, `FTP`, `SFTP`, `SSH`, `TLS`, `RPC`, `CAN`, `QUIC` |
| gRPC             | Preserve the conventional mixed-case spelling                                                    |
| Async APIs       | Use `Async` suffix and `Task<T>`                                                                 |
| Cancellation     | Accept `CancellationToken`                                                                       |
| Timeouts         | Separate connection, operation, and total deadlines                                              |
| Security         | Authenticate endpoints and validate certificates by default                                      |
| Resources        | Implement `IDisposable` and `IAsyncDisposable`                                                   |
| Buffers          | Use `Robix.Buffers` abstractions                                                                 |
| Streams          | Use `Robix.IO.Streams` abstractions                                                              |
| Errors           | Typed error codes and recoverable results                                                        |
| Platform support | Capability-gated native features                                                                 |
| Observability    | Connection metrics, tracing, and redacted logs                                                   |

## **26. Recommended package dependency architecture**

| Package                   | Main dependencies                                                                |
| ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Robix.Network`           | `Robix.Core`, `Robix.Async`, `Robix.IO`                                          |
| `Robix.Network.HTTP`      | `Robix.Network.TCP`, `Robix.Network.TLS`, `Robix.Network.QUIC` as supported      |
| `Robix.Network.HTTPS`     | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, `Robix.Network.TLS`                                        |
| `Robix.Network.WebSocket` | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                    |
| `Robix.Network.TCP`       | `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                                          |
| `Robix.Network.UDP`       | `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                                          |
| `Robix.Network.Sockets`   | `Robix.Network`, native OS backends                                              |
| `Robix.Network.DNS`       | `Robix.Network.UDP`, `Robix.Network.TCP` and optional secure resolver transports |
| `Robix.Network.FTP`       | `Robix.Network.TCP`                                                              |
| `Robix.Network.SFTP`      | `Robix.Network.SSH`                                                              |
| `Robix.Network.SSH`       | `Robix.Network.TCP`, `Robix.Security.Cryptography`                               |
| `Robix.Network.Proxy`     | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                    |
| `Robix.Network.TLS`       | `Robix.Security.Cryptography`, `Robix.Network.TCP` for stream adapters           |
| `Robix.Network.Email`     | `Robix.Network.TCP`, `Robix.Network.TLS`, `Robix.MIME`                           |
| `Robix.Network.Messaging` | Network transports and message codecs                                            |
| `Robix.Network.RPC`       | `Robix.Network`, `Robix.Serialization`                                           |
| `Robix.Network.gRPC`      | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, Protocol Buffers                                           |
| `Robix.Network.Discovery` | `Robix.Network.DNS`, `Robix.Network.Multicast`                                   |
| `Robix.Network.Multicast` | `Robix.Network.UDP`                                                              |
| `Robix.Network.CAN`       | `Robix.Hardware.CAN`, platform drivers                                           |
| `Robix.Network.QUIC`      | UDP transport, TLS 1.3 cryptographic handshake backend                           |

These relationships are conceptual. They should not introduce circular module dependencies; common connection, certificate, and transport interfaces should live in foundational modules.

## **27. Example: Strictly typed HTTP application**

```
Package Robotics.NetworkClient;


Import Robix.Network.HTTP As HTTP;
Import Robix.JSON As JSON;
Import Robix.Cancellation;
Import Robix.Results;

Public Async Function FetchRobotStatus(
    String url,
    CancellationToken cancellation
) -> Task<Result<RobotStatus, NetworkError>> {

    HTTPClient client = HTTP.CreateClient();

    Try {
        HTTPResponse response = Await client.GetAsync(
            url,
            cancellation
        );

        response.EnsureSuccess();

        RobotStatus status =
            Await response.ReadJSONAsync<RobotStatus>();

        Return Success(status);
    }
    Catch (NetworkError error) {
        Return Failure(error);
    }
    Finally {
        Await client.DisposeAsync();
    }
}
```

This is illustrative Robix syntax. In the finalized implementation, HTTP status failures, JSON parsing failures, cancellation, and disposal behavior should be modeled explicitly in the function's declared error contract.

# **28. Important architectural decisions**

Before freezing these 21 packages, Robix should settle the following:

1. Whether all socket operations use a unified event-loop scheduler.
2. Whether asynchronous methods return `Task<T>` or `Promise<T>` as their canonical public type.
3. Whether networking APIs use exceptions, typed `Result<T,E>`, or both.
4. Whether HTTP/3 and QUIC are bundled by default or require an optional native backend.
5. Whether TLS uses an operating-system cryptographic provider or a separately maintained library.
6. How buffers and streams are owned during asynchronous operations.
7. How cancellation interacts with partial reads, sends, and resource cleanup.
8. Which protocols support automatic reconnection.
9. Whether message framing is part of a common transport interface.
10. How network identity and permissions are enforced for embedded and robotics systems.
11. Which operations are supported on Windows, Linux, macOS, and embedded targets.
12. How to handle API versioning as protocols and security requirements evolve.

## **Final recommendation**

The most maintainable implementation is a layered architecture:

**Application protocols**

HTTP · HTTPS · WebSocket · Email · RPC · gRPC · Messaging · SFTP

**Transport security and services**

TLS · SSH · DNS · Proxy · Discovery

**Transport protocols**

TCP · UDP · QUIC · Multicast · CAN

**Foundational runtime**

Sockets · Streams · Buffers · Async · Cancellation · OS / Hardware backends

## 7. Operating system and system programming

# **ROBIX — Operating System and System Programming API Specification**

21 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Async APIs · Native interoperability · System security · Cross-platform support

This is the proposed **Robix Operating System and System Programming Standard Library (ROSSL)**, following the same detailed approach as the previous Core, Mathematics, Algorithms, Filesystem, and Networking specifications.

It covers all **21 packages** you listed, including proposed classes, interfaces, enumerations, functions, platform-specific capabilities, error handling, and implementation rules.

The objective is to make Robix suitable for developing command-line applications, desktop utilities, operating-system tools, service managers, process supervisors, embedded systems, hardware-facing applications, and native integrations.

### **Required design principles**

- **Strict type safety:** All official compiled packages support strict typing.
- **Cross-platform abstractions:** Separate portable system APIs from Windows, Linux, macOS, and other platform backends.
- **Secure permissions:** Privileged operations must have explicit authorization and report permission failures.
- **Async operations:** Process execution, service control, system monitoring, and terminal I/O support asynchronous methods.
- **Resource management:** Native handles, processes, services, and terminal resources support deterministic disposal.
- **Native interoperability:** Foreign types and pointers must have explicit ownership, alignment, lifetime, and calling-convention rules.
- **Predictable errors:** APIs expose typed errors and meaningful platform capability information.
- **No hidden privilege escalation:** System APIs operate only with the calling application's effective permissions.

These are proposed Robix APIs, not claims that the language currently implements every listed feature. Their final signatures will depend on Robix's compiler, runtime, memory model, and supported platforms.

```

```

**Process monitoring**

```

```

**Supervision**

```

```

**Execution limits**

```

Processes.SetCPULimit(id, limit)
Processes.SetExecutionTimeout(id, duration)
Processes.SetOpenHandleLimit(id, count)
Processes.SetProcessGroup(id, group)
```

Process metrics and limits should report platform capabilities. Process identifiers may be reused, so process handles and identity checks must distinguish one process instance from another.

**Validation and auditing**

```

Permissions.Compare(a, b)
Permissions.GetMissing(required, available)
Permissions.Require(permission)
Permissions.AuditAccess(resource, action)
Permissions.GetPermissionDiagnostics()
```

Robix should use **least privilege by default**. A method called `Grant` or `EnablePrivilege` must never bypass the OS security model; it can only perform an authorized operation.

## **7.&#x20;**`Robix.System.Signals`

### **Classes and types**

- `Signal`
- `SignalNumber`

- `SignalType`
- `SignalInfo`
- `SignalContext`

- `SignalManager`
- `SignalSubscription`
- `SignalRegistration`
- `SignalQueue`
- `SignalWaiter`
- `SignalOptions`
- `SignalCapabilities`
- `SignalError`
- `SignalPermissionError`
- `SignalNotSupportedError`
- `GracefulShutdownSignal`

**Common signal constants**

```
SIGINT
SIGTERM
SIGHUP
SIGQUIT
SIGUSR1
SIGUSR2
SIGCHLD
SIGPIPE
SIGALRM
SIGWINCH
SIGSTOP
SIGCONT
```

Signal availability varies by operating system. Some signals cannot be intercepted or ignored.

### **Functions**

**Registration**

```
Signals.Register(signal, handler)
Signals.Unregister(registration)
Signals.Subscribe(signal, handler)
Signals.Unsubscribe(subscription)
Signals.SetHandler(signal, handler)

```

**Sending**

```

```

**Waiting**

```

Signals.WaitAny(signalSet)

Signals.WaitWithTimeout(signalSet, duration)
```

**Signal masks**

```

Signals.Unblock(signal)
Signals.SetMask(mask)
Signals.GetMask()

Signals.RemoveFromMask(mask, signal)
```

**Inspection**

```
Signals.GetName(signal)
Signals.GetNumber(name)
Signals.GetInfo(signal)
Signals.IsSupported(signal)
Signals.IsCatchable(signal)
Signals.IsBlocked(signal)
Signals.ListSupported()
```

**Graceful shutdown**

```
Signals.OnInterrupt(callback)
Signals.OnTermination(callback)
Signals.OnHangup(callback)
Signals.RegisterShutdownHandler(handler)
Signals.CreateShutdownToken(signals)
Signals.WaitForShutdownAsync()
```

**Critical safety rule:** Low-level signal handlers must not execute arbitrary Robix code directly in an asynchronous signal context. The runtime should convert signals into safely queued events handled by its scheduler or event loop.

**Security:** `Run(executable, arguments)` should execute directly without an implicit shell. Shell execution must be an explicit separate API because command strings and argument arrays have fundamentally different injection risks.

**Performance:** Monitoring should avoid excessive polling and provide adjustable sampling rates. Telemetry should redact secrets and avoid collecting personally sensitive data without an explicit reason.

## **12.&#x20;**`Robix.System.Power`

### **Classes and types**

**Important:** Power-state changes can terminate or suspend work abruptly. APIs should expose appropriate permissions and never promise that a sleep, shutdown, or wake request will succeed on every platform.

## **13.&#x20;**`Robix.System.Registry`

**Responsibility:** Windows Registry operations, registry keys, values, permissions, and change monitoring.

This package should be **Windows-specific**. Other platforms may offer different configuration stores, but they should not pretend to implement Windows Registry semantics.

### **Classes and types**

- `Registry`
- `RegistryKey`
- `RegistryKeyHandle`
- `RegistryRoot`
- `RegistryHive`
- `RegistryView`
- `RegistryPath`
- `RegistryValue`

- `RegistryExport`
- `RegistryImport`
- `RegistryStatistics`
- `RegistryError`
- `RegistryKeyNotFoundError`
- `RegistryAccessDeniedError`
- `RegistryTypeMismatchError`

### **Common registry roots**

```
HKEY_CLASSES_ROOT
HKEY_CURRENT_USER
HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE
HKEY_USERS
HKEY_CURRENT_CONFIG
```

### **Functions**

**Opening keys**

```

```

**Reading**

```

```

**Writing**

```

```

**Key management**

```

```

**Security**

```

```

**Monitoring**

```
Registry.WatchKey(key)
Registry.WatchKeyAsync(key)
Registry.OnKeyChanged(key, callback)
Registry.StopWatching(watcher)
```

**Import/export**

```
Registry.ExportKey(key, path)
Registry.ImportKey(path)
Registry.SaveHive(key, path)
Registry.LoadHive(path, options)
```

**Platform rules:** Unsupported platforms should return a clear `PlatformNotSupportedError`. Registry virtualization, 32-bit/64-bit views, remote access, and transactions are conditional features.

**Safety:** Native functions must have explicit calling conventions, argument layouts, pointer ownership, and lifetime requirements. Raw system calls should not be presented as portable high-level functionality.

## **15.&#x20;**`Robix.Environment`

**Responsibility:** Robix application execution environment, runtime configuration, application directories, feature flags, deployment environment, and application context.

This differs from `Robix.System.Environment`: the system package manages OS process environment variables; this package provides a coherent **Robix application-level environment**.

```

**Security:** Production-mode checks are configuration signals, not security boundaries. Permission checks must rely on real authorization rather than a boolean environment flag.

```

**CPU features**

```
Native.GetCPUFeatures()
Native.SupportsSIMD()
Native.SupportsAVX()
Native.SupportsAVX2()
Native.SupportsNEON()
Native.GetCacheLineSize()
Native.GetNativeWordSize()
```

**Atomic operations**

```
Native.AtomicLoad(pointer, order)
Native.AtomicStore(pointer, value, order)
Native.AtomicExchange(pointer, value, order)
Native.AtomicCompareExchange(pointer, expected, replacement, order)
Native.AtomicFetchAdd(pointer, value, order)
Native.MemoryFence(order)
```

**Callbacks**

```
Native.CreateCallback(delegate, signature)
Native.GetCallbackPointer(callback)
Native.ReleaseCallback(callback)
```

**Mandatory restrictions:** Raw pointers, unchecked memory access, native function invocation, and handle ownership transfer should require an explicit unsafe capability or language construct. Native resource ownership must never be ambiguous.

## **20.&#x20;**`Robix.FFI`

**Responsibility:** Foreign Function Interface declarations, cross-language calls, ABI compatibility, marshalling, native callbacks, struct layouts, and interoperability safety.

### **Functions**

**Foreign library management**

```
FFI.LoadLibrary(path)

FFI.ResolveLibrary(name)
```

**Foreign calls**

```
FFI.Call(function, arguments)

```

**Type mapping**

```
FFI.MapType<T>(foreignType)

FFI.GetStructLayout(type)
FFI.GetUnionLayout(type)
FFI.CheckABICompatibility(type, abi)
```

**Marshalling**

```
FFI.Marshal<T>(value)
FFI.Unmarshal<T>(foreignValue)
FFI.MarshalArray<T>(values)
FFI.UnmarshalArray<T>(pointer, count)
FFI.MarshalString(text, encoding)
FFI.UnmarshalString(pointer, length, encoding)
FFI.MarshalStruct<T>(value)
FFI.UnmarshalStruct<T>(pointer)
```

**Memory ownership**

```
FFI.AllocateForeign(size, allocator)
FFI.FreeForeign(pointer, allocator)
FFI.WrapForeignPointer<T>(pointer, ownership)
FFI.BorrowForeignPointer<T>(pointer, lifetime)
FFI.CopyFromForeign(pointer, length)
FFI.CopyToForeign(pointer, bytes)
```

**Callbacks**

```
FFI.CreateCallback(delegate, signature)
FFI.RegisterCallback(function, callback)
FFI.GetCallbackPointer(callback)
FFI.ReleaseCallback(callback)
```

**Bindings**

```
FFI.ParseCHeader(path)
FFI.GenerateBindings(header, options)
FFI.GenerateTypeDeclarations(header)
FFI.GenerateFunctionDeclarations(header)
FFI.ValidateBindings(bindings)
FFI.GetBindingDiagnostics(bindings)
```

**ABI inspection**

```
FFI.GetCurrentABI()
FFI.GetCallingConventions()
FFI.IsCallingConventionSupported(convention)
FFI.ValidateFunctionSignature(signature)
FFI.ValidateStructLayout(layout)
```

**Illustrative FFI declaration**

```
Package NativeInteropExample;


Import Robix.FFI;

External Library "native_math" {

    CallingConvention CDecl;

    Public External Function native_add(
        Int32 a,
        Int32 b
    ) -> Int32;
}

Public Function Add(Int32 a, Int32 b) -> Int32 {
    Return native_add(a, b);
}
```

**FFI safety requirements**

- Every imported function must have an explicit ABI and calling convention.
- Pointers must be typed and assigned ownership rules.
- Foreign callbacks must remain alive for the duration required by the foreign library.
- Foreign exceptions must not cross incompatible ABI boundaries.
- Native strings must use a declared encoding.
- Struct padding, alignment, and field offsets must be deterministic.
- Blocking native calls must not unintentionally block the Robix asynchronous scheduler.

## **21.&#x20;**`Robix.Interop`

**Responsibility:** High-level interoperability with other languages, runtimes, applications, components, and data representations.

`Robix.FFI` handles native calling conventions and memory layouts. `Robix.Interop` should provide higher-level adapters for complete foreign objects, runtime environments, modules, and language-specific integrations.
